| `20260813.060935` | [`20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-r1.bron`](20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-r1.bron) | **Mycelium Tenure r1 — a contested name is decided by the agreed order; the namespace earns its verdict** — the Cord commits, gossip converges, batching packs, the Knot checkpoints, and the Ledger Voice reads a lawful supply — five ways to write, agree, and read the mesh, none answering the one question a namespace exists for: when two honest keepers reach for the same name at once, who holds it? A supply total is commutative; a name is not, so the moment two keepers each sign a reservation for `bandun`, the ledger must pick one, and every honest node must pick the **same** one. Self-approved design read `active-designing/20260813-060935_mycelium-tenure-contested-name-exploration.md` names the four-round arc; this round seats the exploration + r1. `mycelium/tenure.rye` reads the Cord's agreed order as the namespace's **verdict** — the first reservation of a name in that order holds it, every later one loses. **The crux is a distinction `fold.fold_log` cannot draw:** it refuses the WHOLE log on `StarTaken`, which would let one node break the ledger by front-running a name; consensus exists so a front-run merely **loses**. So the Tenure walks the committed facts through `fold.fold_fact` (which returns before mutating on `StarTaken`, verified), treating a name-contest loss as a **lawful no-op** the verdict records, and re-raising `IdentityRefused`/`UnknownKind`/`Overdraw` as poison (corruption, not a lost race — `fold_fact` already checks taken-before-affordable). Proven on metal: two keepers reserve `bandun` at round 1; the agreed order grants it to exactly one — the **same** keeper across three arrival permutations (deterministic verdict, the winner computed independently in-test by the Cord's own tie-break, not hardcoded); the loser is a lawful no-op (uncontested `vega` still holds, supply folds to **800**, the loser paid nothing, the loss recorded); a block carrying a broken-fact signature refuses `IdentityRefused` and a free-name overdraw refuses `Overdraw` — corruption still poisons whole. GREEN first pass; witness `tools/mycelium_tenure_witness.rish` GREEN (the Cord and the fold both re-run their own green binaries at its door), TAME bans clean, width-check exit 0. Additive — composes `cord`+`fold` public API only, editing no elder (named per `comlink-tendency`: a name's *tenure* is the term on which it is held; grepped clean in the code namespace); demo keeper seeds — no key/funds/network/custody. Scope kept narrow on purpose: only a name-contest loss is a lawful no-op this round; a double-spend loser is a named horizon. **Next: Tenure r2 — the verdict travels as a `format tenure-v1` Bron record round-tripped byte-for-byte (same `holder_of`), then r3 across a Knot, r4 a real contested fixture cross-checked by awk. Quorum-served verdicts + breach renames (cairn-first) + deep debride + seed force-push stay Keaton's hand.** |

| `20260813.052619` | [`20260813-052619_mycelium-cord-knot-r3-bound-win.bron`](20260813-052619_mycelium-cord-knot-r3-bound-win.bron) | **Mycelium Knot r3 — checkpointing is a genuine bound win; a bounded Dag runs past its 256-block ceiling** — r1 proved a checkpoint folds identical to the whole history, r2 let it travel; r3 measures what the Knot buys, in the Two Rooms voice. The honest checkable claim: the Cord's Dag is statically allocated (`cord_max_blocks` 256), so a history past the ceiling cannot live in one Dag — `mycelium/cord_knot_bench.rye` proves it (a fresh Dag filled to 256 refuses the next block `DagFull`). The win: a checkpoint chain of 5 windows of 64 blocks (**320 facts, past the ceiling**) — each epoch a bounded Cord Dag sealed to a Knot then pruned, the next epoch continuing onto the Knot via `continue_verified` — folds to the **byte-identical** whole-history supply (issued 320) while the **live Dag never exceeds one window (64)**. Ground truth is a plain fold of all 320 issues from empty; `states_match` confirms the chain equals it. Two Rooms honesty: asserts only the exact facts (`total_facts > dag_ceiling`, the ceiling refuses `DagFull`, final supply == ground, `max_live == window < ceiling`) and **reports** the amortization as a measured `loom` line in the binary's output (`total_facts=320 dag_ceiling=256 max_live_blocks=64 windows=5 live_ratio_x10=50 would_overflow=yes` — 5.0x fewer live blocks, which the ceiling makes not merely costly but impossible); a wall-time benchmark stays a named later round. GREEN first pass; witness `tools/mycelium_cord_knot_bench_witness.rish` GREEN (r1 and r2 both re-run GREEN at its door), TAME bans clean, width-check exit 0. Additive — composes `cord`+`fold`+`cord_knot` public API only; demo keeper seeds — no key/funds/network/custody. **Next: Knot r4 — read a real knotted fixture true (a reproducibly-generated `format cord-knot-v1` record `@embedFile`-bound, its recovered supply cross-checked against an independent awk reading, two tools one answer), closing the Knot arc. Breach renames (cairn-first) + deep debride + seed force-push stay Keaton's hand.** |
| `20260813.052102` | [`20260813-052102_mycelium-cord-knot-r2-travels.bron`](20260813-052102_mycelium-cord-knot-r2-travels.bron) | **Mycelium Knot r2 — the Knot travels as a `format cord-knot-v1` record; a tampered checkpoint never crosses as trusted** — r1 sealed a committed Cord to a Knot and proved a continuation folds identical to the whole history; a checkpoint a node cannot hand to another node is only a local optimization, so r2 lets a Knot cross as readable bytes for a far node that never held the pruned prefix. A Knot is a single record (issued · taxed · one `star` line per reserved star in seal order · the digest as hex), far simpler to travel than a Dag. `mycelium/cord_knot_bron.rye` `render` writes it; `parse` reconstructs a Knot through an out-pointer (a Knot holds a full 36 KB `FoldState`, so no return-by-value copy — the batch-bron stack-safety idiom). The parse does **not** recompute the digest, so a tampered field survives into a Knot whose stored digest no longer matches its state — `continue_verified` catches it. Star names are hex-encoded (matching `cord_bron`); a v1 record carries at most `max_stars_in_record` (64) stars, a Knot holding more refusing `TooManyStars` rather than travel truncated. **The crux:** render → parse recovers a Knot that verifies and carries the byte-identical sealed supply (`states_match` over issued·taxed·the whole star set, 700 over one star), continues to the identical supply as the original (fold a fresh 300 issue onto each → both issued 1300), and re-rendering is a **fixed point** (byte-equal). A tampered star line breaks the digest → `continue_verified` refuses `KnotCorrupt`; a bad header (`BadFormat`), an unknown field (`UnknownField`, never dropped), and a bad hex star (`BadHex`) each refuse. GREEN first pass; witness `tools/mycelium_cord_knot_bron_witness.rish` GREEN (the r1 Knot re-runs GREEN at its door), TAME bans clean, width-check exit 0. Additive — composes `cord_knot`+`fold` public API only; demo keeper seeds — no key/funds/network/custody; a quorum-agreed checkpoint stays the Comlink-served serve gate. **Next: Knot r3 — the measured bound-win loom (a history longer than `cord_max_blocks` that only checkpoint-and-prune can carry, an honest ratio never a fabricated benchmark, Two Rooms); then r4 reads a real knotted fixture true. Breach renames (cairn-first) + deep debride + seed force-push stay Keaton's hand.** |
| `20260813.051440` | [`20260813-051440_mycelium-cord-knot-r1-checkpoint.bron`](20260813-051440_mycelium-cord-knot-r1-checkpoint.bron) | **Mycelium Knot r1 — a bounded Cord ties off its committed past; a checkpoint folds identical to the whole history** — with the Cord, gossip-convergence, and fact-batching arcs all closed, the next Lindy-first crux on the Mycelium road is the one durable capability the Cord still lacked: its `Dag` is statically allocated (`cord_max_blocks` 256) — a TigerBeetle feature — yet a bound reached is a mesh that stops. Every long-lived deterministic state machine checkpoints: fold the committed past into a summary, prune it, continue from the summary as if from genesis. Self-approved design read `active-designing/20260813-050903_mycelium-cord-knot-checkpoint-exploration.md` names it the **Knot** (`comlink-tendency`: a knot ties off a length of cord and begins the next; grepped zero collisions), grown as a **sibling** (`mycelium/cord_knot.rye`) keeping `cord.rye` and `fold.rye` green binaries. A `Knot` seals a committed Cord's `fold.FoldState` (issued · taxed · every reserved star) under a streaming SHA-256 digest — no signature (a Knot is a deterministic function of the committed Dag; the digest is integrity-in-travel, never authority). `seal_dag` commits + flattens + folds + seals; `continue_verified` folds a next-epoch log onto a verified Knot. **The crux on metal:** a prefix Dag sealed to a Knot, a fresh suffix epoch continued onto it, folds to the **byte-identical supply** (issued 1000 · taxed 300 · supply 700 · one star `vega`) as the whole history from empty — checked by a deep `states_match` (issued·taxed·the whole reserved-star set, not just count). Both boundaries hold (the Knot at genesis · the full-prefix Knot). **Every fold invariant crosses the cut**, because `fold_fact` carries the supply law in the state it mutates: a corrupted Knot refuses `KnotCorrupt`, a suffix tax of 800 past a checkpointed supply of 700 refuses `Overdraw`, a suffix star already sealed (`vega`) refuses `StarTaken` — the cross-cut facts built raw (not via `append_fact`, which probe-folds from empty and would itself refuse them), sharpening that they are lawful only against the checkpointed supply. One red caught in-round: `append_fact` returns a `FoldState` (probe-folds), not void. Witness `tools/mycelium_cord_knot_witness.rish` GREEN (elder Cord + Mycelium fold both re-run GREEN at its door), TAME bans clean, width-check exit 0. Additive — composes `cord`+`fold` public API only; demo keeper seeds — no key/funds/network/custody; a quorum-agreed checkpoint (a node pruning on a checkpoint it did not compute) reaches the Comlink-served serve gate, Keaton's hand. **Next: Knot r2 — the knot travels as a `format cord-knot-v1` record byte-for-byte, the recovered Knot continuing to the identical supply; then r3 the measured bound-win loom, r4 reads a real knotted fixture true. Breach renames (cairn-first) + deep debride + seed force-push stay Keaton's hand.** |

| `20260813.045555` | [`20260813-045555_mycelium-cord-batch-r3-throughput.bron`](20260813-045555_mycelium-cord-batch-r3-throughput.bron) | **Mycelium fact batching r3 — batching is a pure throughput win, measured honestly as a structural block-count ratio** — r1 proved a batched history folds to the byte-identical supply as the same facts one-per-block; r2 let it travel; r3 measures what batching bought. `mycelium/cord_batch_bench.rye` arranges a fixed corpus of **32** genesis-issue facts two ways — one fact per block across the elder Cord (**32 blocks**) and packed into full batches of 16 across the batched Cord (**2 blocks**) — both folding to the **identical supply** (320). | `20260813.032851` | [`20260813-032851_mycelium-consensus-cord-design-read.bron`](20260813-032851_mycelium-consensus-cord-design-read.bron) | **Mycelium consensus design read — the Cord that bundles many threads into one agreed order** — the constel dev-net ledger closed (r4 reads true); REMEMBER's live edge named the next Lindy-first crux plainly: the Mycelium consensus design read over TigerBeetle + Mysticeti. This self-approved round reads that design and fixes the shape, the name, and the four-round arc — seating no code, so the round that follows opens a spine already known correct. The crux, in one line: **from a mesh of Kumara-signed fact-blocks that reference one another, every honest node derives the identical total order — without a leader, without a certificate round, every buffer bounded — and `mycelium/fold`'s supply law runs over that order unchanged.** Two ancestors thanked clean-room: from **TigerBeetle**, determinism + static allocation (the fold already keeps it — a pure fold over signed facts, static log 1024); from **Mysticeti** (Mysten Labs, the Bullshark/Narwhal lineage, studied under Sui's permissive license), order read from an **uncertified DAG** by a deterministic commit rule, no extra ordering messages. | `20260812.213700` | [`20260812-213700_dahl-j12r2-data-dignity-signed.bron`](20260812-213700_dahl-j12r2-data-dignity-signed.bron) | **DAHL-J12r2 — a succession whose two proofs cannot be forged** — rung one fixed succession as a pure two-proof state machine, yet a bare state machine trusts whoever calls it: a steward could seal a directive in a departed keeper's name, or record an acceptance the heir never gave. `pond/apps/data_dignity_signed.rye` binds **each of the two proofs** to a settled **Kumara identity** — the keeper's seal to the keeper's key, the heir's acceptance to the heir's key. The keeper's directive is signed over a tag (so a seal is never replayed as another act), the estate id (so a seal cannot cross estates), the keeper and heir points, and a **digest of the will's own marks** (a SHA-256 over every willed item and its disposition, in order). Binding the digest is the honesty crux: `seal_signed` recomputes the digest from the estate's **current** marks and checks the keeper's signature over it, so alter a bequeathed mark to rested (or slip a holding in) after the keeper signed and the seal falls `BadConsent` — a steward cannot rewrite the will a departed keeper meant. The heir's acceptance is signed over a tag, the estate id, and the two points, so it cannot be lifted from one estate to another. A `Keyring` maps a point to its keeper key (the caller's statement of whose word to trust, as `council_sky_signed` maps a seat to its key); `verify_directive`/`verify_acceptance` touch no estate, so a traveled record (r3) can re-check offline. Refusals hold — a tampered will (`BadConsent`, never sealed), a forged directive or forged acceptance by a stranger (`BadConsent`), an unenrolled party (`UnknownKeeper`, before the estate is touched), a cross-estate signature (`BadConsent`). Succession itself is rung one's unchanged two-proof gate, so a signed round still needs both a sealed directive and an acceptance. Additive — rung one grew only two pub readers (`item_at`, `disposition_at`) so r2 can digest the marks; r1 re-runs GREEN. Two build reds caught on metal (a param shadowing a file-scope demo const; the streaming SHA-256 method is `final`, not `finalize`) and fixed same-round. Demo keeper and heir seeds only — no real key, no network, no real seed (the maintainer's own succession is the maintainer's hand — gate #4-adjacent). Witness `tools/dahl_j12_data_dignity_signed_witness.rish` GREEN (5 selftests + r1 re-run), TAME bans + width clean. **Next Lindy-first crux: DAHL-J12r3 — the signed estate travels as a `format data-dignity-v1` Bron record and parses back byte-for-byte, still dignity-honest offline** |
| `20260812.212607` | [`20260812-212607_dahl-j12r1-data-dignity.bron`](20260812-212607_dahl-j12r1-data-dignity.bron) | **DAHL-J12r1 — data_dignity, succession honors the keeper's declared word (Journey 12 opens)** — with Journey 11 (Council skies) closed, the next Lindy-first crux on the whole 1,024-round road is **Season 3 Journey 12 (Data-dignity)**: Commons' last and most tender crux — what becomes of a keeper's data when the keeper is gone (self-approved design round `active-designing/20260812-212607_…`). Journeys 9–11 built how *living* keepers meet; Journey 12 carries consent, and the keeper's own word, past a single lifetime. `pond/apps/data_dignity.rye` fixes the crux — **succession honors the keeper's declared word, and rests what the keeper chose to rest.** An `Estate` opens naming a keeper and a distinct heir (`SelfHeir` refused); the keeper wills each holding **bequeathed** (passes to the heir) or **rested** (the keeper's dignity choice — extinguished at succession, inherited by no one), **seals** the directive, and the heir **accepts**. Succession fires only on **two proofs, never one** — the sealed directive (`NotSealed`) *and* the heir's acceptance (`NotAccepted`) — so neither the departing keeper's word alone nor the receiving heir's alone moves custody: this is the ledger's escape law (both consent, no veto) carried across the one boundary it had not crossed, the keeper's absence. Who holds an item is **derived**, never written — before succession every willed item is the keeper's; after, a bequeathed item is the heir's and a rested item is `rested` (no one's). The rested set's extinction is the promise made checkable: `heir_held_count == bequeathed_count` (asserted in `succeed`), and no rested item is ever the heir's — the one preference a departed person can no longer defend is the one the rule guards hardest. Refusals hold — a self-heir (`SelfHeir`), a re-willed item (`AlreadyWilled`), a word after seal (`Sealed`), a replayed succession (`AlreadySucceeded`). Pure state machine — no signing yet (r2 signs the keeper's seal and the heir's acceptance with settled Kumara identities, mirroring `skate_group_signed`; r3 travels as Bron; r4 reads a real fixture true). Facts only, custody-first: no network, no funds, no key, no real seed (the maintainer's own succession over a real Kumara instance is the maintainer's hand — gate #4-adjacent); demo keeper and heir points only. The honesty crux mirrors the group's relabel — flip a ballot's yes to no after signing and reuse the signature, and it falls `BadConsent` (the signature was over the choice actually made); a forged ballot by the wrong keeper (`BadConsent`, tally unmoved), a cross-council signature (`BadConsent`) each refuse. And the odd-quorum lone-hand guard carries through r1 unchanged — a seat's valid signature lets it vote once per proposal, the repeat refusing `AlreadyVoted` with the tally held, so signing still cannot fake a majority. Additive — composes `council_sky` (r1) and `kumara` over public API (r1 re-runs GREEN); demo keeper seeds only, no real key, no network (serve reaches the custody gate). Witness `tools/dahl_j11_council_sky_signed_witness.rish` GREEN (2 welcomes + 4 refusals + r1 re-run), TAME bans + width clean. **Next Lindy-first crux: DAHL-J11r3 — the signed council travels as a `format council-sky-v1` Bron record and parses back byte-for-byte, still tie-free and quorum-honest offline** |
| `20260812.210952` | [`20260812-210952_dahl-j11r1-council-sky-odd-quorum.bron`](20260812-210952_dahl-j11r1-council-sky-odd-quorum.bron) | **DAHL-J11r1 — council loads a sky; the odd fractal gives a tie-free quorum and complete triads (Journey 11 opens)** — with Journey 10 (Membership) closed, the next Lindy-first crux on the whole 1,024-round road is **Season 3 Journey 11 (Council skies)**: the loadable-topologies brief's load-bearing move — a community does not merely pick a threshold (J10), it **loads a whole shape**, a *sky*, the way a console loads a game, and the sky's own odd fractal *gives* the council its tie-free quorum and its roles (self-approved design round `active-designing/20260812-210603_…`). `pond/apps/council_sky.rye` composes `comlink/topology.rye`'s `Sky` — a `Council` loads a sky and seats a body at one of the sky's own fractal levels (`galaxies · stars · planets · prosperity`). The **council sky's** four counts are **3 · 9 · 15 · 27**, the odd-quorum fractal, every one odd; the quorum is the strict majority of the odd body (`size/2+1`), and because the body is odd, `2·quorum > size` (invariant-asserted) — yes and no can never both reach the quorum, so a fully-voted proposal is **always decided, never a tie**. The civic **`compass_sky` is not a council sky**: its `prosperity` is 60 — even — so seating its d60 refuses `EvenCouncil` (its odd star level 5 still seats, quorum 3). Each seat wears a **modality role** — `initiator · sustainer · adapter` by `seat mod 3` — a pure function of position, so any triad is complete (one to open, one to hold, one to bridge) and can never drift from the seated body. The outcome is **derived** from the tally, never written. Refusals hold: a size the sky does not name (`NotAFractalLevel`), an even level (`EvenCouncil`), a non-seat vote (`NotSeated`), a doubled ballot (`AlreadyVoted`, tally unmoved — one hand cannot fake a majority). Pure state machine — no signing yet (r2 signs each vote with the member's Kumara identity mirroring `skate_group_signed`; r3 travels as Bron; r4 reads a real fixture true). Facts only, custody-first: no network, no key, no real identity (a council served over the wire reaches the serve gate — Keaton's hand); demo seats only. A compound `assert(a and b)` caught by `tame_style_check` before commit and split into a plain bool. Witness `tools/dahl_j11_council_sky_witness.rish` GREEN (6 cases), TAME bans + width clean. **Next Lindy-first crux: DAHL-J11r2 — each vote signed by the member's settled Kumara identity, so a steward cannot fabricate a council vote** |

| `20260812.201611` | [`20260812-201611_dahl-j9r1-skate-circle-consent.bron`](20260812-201611_dahl-j9r1-skate-circle-consent.bron) | **DAHL-J9r1 — Skate, consent is mutual, presence is never default (Season 3 opens)** — with Seasons 1 (TACT) and 2 (Yield) complete across every agent-doable rung, the next Lindy-first crux on the whole 1,024-round road is Season 3 (Commons, DAHL), Journey 9 (Skate): the social layer over Pond identities, *a shared surface entered by consent, never by default* (self-approved design round `active-designing/20260812-201611_…`). `pond/apps/skate_circle.rye` carries Pond's own customs trichotomy (place · hold · refuse) to the social border, where the resting verdict is the strictest — a `Circle` a keeper joins only by their own explicit act (no member by default), and a link that is **mutual-only**: one keeper's `request_link` is a standing offer that **holds**, and the connection **places** (links) only when the other has also asked, so a one-sided request holds forever and a steward cannot manufacture a connection from one side. A link is not stored but **derived** as the presence of two opposing requests, so the mutual-only law cannot be bypassed by writing a link directly; `visible` follows `linked` exactly (invariant-asserted), so presence is never default — a joined keeper who linked no one sees no one and is seen by no one. Every refusal holds: a link touching a non-member (`NotMember`), a self-link (`SelfLink`), a taken seat (`AlreadyMember`), a repeated offer (`AlreadyRequested`), a full circle (`CircleFull`). Pure state machine — no signing yet (r2 binds each act to a settled Kumara identity mirroring `commerce_trade_signed`; r3 travels as Bron; r4 reads a real fixture true). Facts only, custody-first: no network, no key, no real identity (a circle served over the wire reaches the serve gate — Keaton's hand); demo keeper points only. Witness `tools/dahl_j9_skate_circle_witness.rish` GREEN (2 welcomes + 4 refusals), TAME bans + width clean. **Next Lindy-first crux: DAHL-J9r2 — each consent act signed by the keeper's settled Kumara identity, so consent cannot be fabricated** |

| `20260812.200035` | [`20260812-200035_gism-ayre-j8r1-entity-books.bron`](20260812-200035_gism-ayre-j8r1-entity-books.bron) | **GISM-AYRE-J8r1 — entity books that balance, never braided (Season 2 Journey 8 opens)** — with AYRE Journey 7 closed, the next Lindy-first crux on the whole 1,024-round road is Season 2's last durable promise for the owning half: **Journey 8, Cash-first** — the accounting spine for Linengrow PBC / Siya Fund. An inter-entity `transfer` books one fact on **both** entities' journals — the grantor debits an expense and credits cash, the grantee debits cash and credits revenue, of the one agreed amount — so the value the grantor parts with equals the value the grantee receives by construction. Two conservation laws hold together: each entity's own books balance, and the pair conserves across the cash line (`pair_conserves` isolates the transfer's cash delta from a pre-snapshot — Siya's cash fell by 1500, Linengrow's rose by 1500, summing to zero). Segregation is proven **byte-identical**: a fact added to Linengrow's journal leaves Siya's fold unchanged account for account — the books are never braided. A `PortableCert` owns its name/point/signature buffers so the `SignedRecord` it rebuilds points into its own storage, never freed text; `still_satisfied` re-verifies every traveled attestation against the recipient's keyring through rung two's own `verify_held`, and re-running rung two's `evaluate_signed` over the traveled bundle yields the identical `Certified`. Witnessed in its own app (`mantra_editor_fork.rye`, matching one-app-per-rung): opening `c.txt` grows the rendered graph by exactly one lane line (`r1`, no arrow), lane count up by one, every existing lane byte-for-byte unchanged; the forked lane then behaves like any lane (a commit chains `r1 -> r2`); opening an existing lane refuses `LaneExists`. Additive — the editor (J4r1) witness re-runs GREEN. Witness `tools/buhr_mantra_editor_fork_witness.rish` GREEN (3 welcomes + editor re-run), TAME + width clean. **The editor's core append surface — open a lane, commit to a lane, show growth lit — is complete** |

**Seasons roster:** [`SEASONS.md`](SEASONS.md)

Living index for the append-only session stream. This table reads **newest first** and holds the **current season day only**. Closed seasons fold into dated indexes under `archive/`; the roster names each season in one line.

**Living format:** **Bron** (`.bron`) — immutable key-value at the seam. Historical Markdown logs are folded under `archive/YYYYMMDD/`. Rules: [`.cursor/rules/session-logs.mdc`](../.cursor/rules/session-logs.mdc) · [`.claude/rules/session-logs.md`](../.claude/rules/session-logs.md).

**Day’s truth is this index’s order, not filename sort.** One-clock stamps sort ascending on disk; trust the table for sequence within a day. Naming: [`context/specs/20260627-102012_one-clock-naming-law.md`](../context/specs/20260627-102012_one-clock-naming-law.md).

**Living pin bound:** `living_pin_max_bytes = 24576` ([spec](../context/specs/20260724-132812_pin-and-ledger-living-pin-max-bytes.md)) · growth law: [`../context/specs/append-only-growth-law.md`](../context/specs/append-only-growth-law.md).

*Erratum `20260724.203617` — UTC window:* four living logs were stamped from `Etc/UTC` and must be read in index order (now on the 20260724/25 season surfaces). Cause fixed: host zone `America/New_York`. From `20260724.205009` the one-clock witness is **blocking**.
